Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.

And I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.

I haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.

And at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.

That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.

Like with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.

Then it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.

It happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.

I know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.

I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.

It isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.

So… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.

I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.

It’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.

So it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.

He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to come in is I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. I don’t mean that I’ve tried to do anything to myself, because I haven’t. But it scared me that the thought keeps coming back, you know? Like I’ll go to bed and part of me thinks maybe it would be easier not to have to get up and keep going. And I don’t like saying that out loud, but that’s really why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about four months now. It started around the time my aunt came to live with us. I didn’t really think of it as a “thing” at first, more like just being worn down and having a bad night here or there, but then I noticed the thought kept coming back. So, yes… about four months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in situations unrelated to your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. That’s where my mind goes straight to, “You’re a bad woman,” or “You should be more grateful,” and then it feels like I’m doing something shameful just by having that tired or annoyed feeling.\n\nIt sounds small when I say it out loud, like just being irritated for a moment, but inside it doesn’t feel small. It turns into this whole judgment about me, like I’m not the kind of family member I should be, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful all the time.\n\nSo if you mean unrelated to her, I don’t think it happens as much. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.

If I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. I don’t just think, “I’m tired.” I think, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then it turns into, “I’m a bad woman. I should be more grateful. I’m bringing shame on my family by not having a better heart.”

So even if things around me are technically fine, that feeling can still come up if it involves her. It makes me feel like there’s something wrong with me as a person, not just that I had a bad moment.

It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in situations unrelated to your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. That’s where my mind goes straight to, “You’re a bad woman,” or “You should be more grateful,” and then it feels like I’m doing something shameful just by having that tired or annoyed feeling.\n\nIt sounds small when I say it out loud, like just being irritated for a moment, but inside it doesn’t feel small. It turns into this whole judgment about me, like I’m not the kind of family member I should be, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful all the time.\n\nSo if you mean unrelated to her, I don’t think it happens as much. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Most of the time? No… I don’t think it’s most of the time when it has nothing to do with her. I don’t walk around every minute picking myself apart about every little thing, not exactly.

It’s more like there’s this one sore place in me, and it gets touched when I’m dealing with my aunt. If I feel tired, or I feel annoyed, even quietly, even if I don’t say anything mean… I start thinking there must be something wrong with me. Like a good woman wouldn’t feel that way. A better person would just be grateful and patient and loving all the time.

And then my mind goes further than it should. It isn’t just, “I’m tired today.” It becomes, “I’m a bad woman,” or, “I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t be more grateful.” So outside of that, not as much. But when it comes to her, yes, I can be very hard on myself, too hard, I guess.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to come in is I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. I don’t mean that I’ve tried to do anything to myself, because I haven’t. But it scared me that the thought keeps coming back, you know? Like I’ll go to bed and part of me thinks maybe it would be easier not to have to get up and keep going. And I don’t like saying that out loud, but that’s really why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about four months now. It started around the time my aunt came to live with us. I didn’t really think of it as a “thing” at first, more like just being worn down and having a bad night here or there, but then I noticed the thought kept coming back. So, yes… about four months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in situations unrelated to your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. That’s where my mind goes straight to, “You’re a bad woman,” or “You should be more grateful,” and then it feels like I’m doing something shameful just by having that tired or annoyed feeling.\n\nIt sounds small when I say it out loud, like just being irritated for a moment, but inside it doesn’t feel small. It turns into this whole judgment about me, like I’m not the kind of family member I should be, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful all the time.\n\nSo if you mean unrelated to her, I don’t think it happens as much. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. I don’t just think, “I’m tired.” I think, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then it turns into, “I’m a bad woman. I should be more grateful. I’m bringing shame on my family by not having a better heart.”\n\nSo even if things around me are technically fine, that feeling can still come up if it involves her. It makes me feel like there’s something wrong with me as a person, not just that I had a bad moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're overly critical of yourself most of the time, even when it's not about your aunt?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? No… I don’t think it’s most of the time when it has nothing to do with her. I don’t walk around every minute picking myself apart about every little thing, not exactly.\n\nIt’s more like there’s this one sore place in me, and it gets touched when I’m dealing with my aunt. If I feel tired, or I feel annoyed, even quietly, even if I don’t say anything mean… I start thinking there must be something wrong with me. Like a good woman wouldn’t feel that way. A better person would just be grateful and patient and loving all the time.\n\nAnd then my mind goes further than it should. It isn’t just, “I’m tired today.” It becomes, “I’m a bad woman,” or, “I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t be more grateful.” So outside of that, not as much. But when it comes to her, yes, I can be very hard on myself, too hard, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really… not if it’s completely separate from my aunt or some situation with her. I know I’ve probably said that a few times, but that really is where it centers for me.

It’s not like I wake up and criticize myself about everything, or sit there thinking I’m terrible for no reason. It’s more that when I feel tired of taking care of her, or I get annoyed even quietly, that’s when it starts. Then I tell myself, “What kind of woman gets annoyed with her aunt?” or “You should be more grateful.” And it turns into this feeling that I’m a bad woman, like I’m bringing shame on my family just because I’m not feeling patient and thankful enough.

So if there’s no connection to her and no particular situation like that, I don’t think I’m overly critical in the same way. It’s really those moments with her that make me judge myself so harshly.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to come in is I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. I don’t mean that I’ve tried to do anything to myself, because I haven’t. But it scared me that the thought keeps coming back, you know? Like I’ll go to bed and part of me thinks maybe it would be easier not to have to get up and keep going. And I don’t like saying that out loud, but that’s really why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about four months now. It started around the time my aunt came to live with us. I didn’t really think of it as a “thing” at first, more like just being worn down and having a bad night here or there, but then I noticed the thought kept coming back. So, yes… about four months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in situations unrelated to your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. It turns into this whole judgment about me, like I’m not the kind of family member I should be, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful all the time.\n\nSo if you mean unrelated to her, I don’t think it happens as much. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not usually when there’s nothing bothering me, no. I don’t think I’m just sitting around all day criticizing myself out of nowhere.

It’s more… it has to get started by that feeling with my aunt. Like if I’m tired and she needs something, or I feel even a little annoyed, then I start turning on myself. I’ll think, “Why am I like this? She’s family. I should be more grateful.” And then it doesn’t stay small. It becomes, “I’m a bad woman,” like there’s something wrong with my character.

And I know maybe other people get tired too, but in my mind it feels shameful. Like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I think I’m supposed to be. So… no, not for no reason exactly. But when that comes up, I can be very hard on myself.

It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. I think… not really in this same way, no. I mean, I’ve felt bad about myself before like anybody does, I suppose, but these particular thoughts — feeling like I’m a bad woman because I’m tired or annoyed with my aunt, and like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t be more grateful — that feels tied to this situation with her.

It’s hard to explain because it’s not just ordinary guilt, like, “Oh, I should have been nicer.” It has this heavier feeling to it, like it says something about who I am as a person. When I get worn down or impatient, even if I don’t do anything, my mind goes straight to, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then I feel ashamed, like I’ve failed somehow.

So if you mean have I had this exact kind of thinking at other times in my life, I don’t think so. Not like this. It really seems to come up around taking care of my aunt and not feeling grateful or patient enough.

---------- turn 15 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F33.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to come in is I’ve been having these thoughts that it might be a relief if I just didn’t wake up. I don’t mean that I’ve tried to do anything to myself, because I haven’t. But it scared me that the thought keeps coming back, you know? Like I’ll go to bed and part of me thinks maybe it would be easier not to have to get up and keep going. And I don’t like saying that out loud, but that’s really why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about four months now. It started around the time my aunt came to live with us. I didn’t really think of it as a “thing” at first, more like just being worn down and having a bad night here or there, but then I noticed the thought kept coming back. So, yes… about four months, give or take.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel not like myself. I used to really look forward to little things, like doing my watercolors. I’d set everything out and fuss with the colors, and even if it wasn’t any good, it made me feel calm. But now the paints and brushes just sit there. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. It’s like the enjoyment is just… gone, or far away.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. And then I think I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t seem to have a better heart about it.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I criticize myself about every little thing in life, but that one feeling spreads. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I don’t sit there going over old mistakes from years ago as much as I get stuck on what happens now with my aunt.\n\nIt’s like, if I’m tired and she needs me, or if I feel annoyed with her for even a moment, then I start turning it into something bigger about who I am. I’ll think, “I’m a bad woman for feeling this. I should be more grateful. She’s family.” And then I start feeling like I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not being patient enough or kind enough.\n\nSo it’s not really past things I’ve done… it’s more that these moments with her make me judge my whole self. Once that feeling starts, it can feel like proof that I’m not the person I should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in situations unrelated to your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can be hard on myself in general, I suppose, but the thing that really takes hold is with my aunt. That’s where my mind goes straight to, “You’re a bad woman,” or “You should be more grateful,” and then it feels like I’m doing something shameful just by having that tired or annoyed feeling.\n\nIt sounds small when I say it out loud, like just being irritated for a moment, but inside it doesn’t feel small. It turns into this whole judgment about me, like I’m not the kind of family member I should be, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful all the time.\n\nSo if you mean unrelated to her, I don’t think it happens as much. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. I don’t just think, “I’m tired.” I think, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then it turns into, “I’m a bad woman. I should be more grateful. I’m bringing shame on my family by not having a better heart.”\n\nSo even if things around me are technically fine, that feeling can still come up if it involves her. It makes me feel like there’s something wrong with me as a person, not just that I had a bad moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're overly critical of yourself most of the time, even when it's not about your aunt?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? No… I don’t think it’s most of the time when it has nothing to do with her. I don’t walk around every minute picking myself apart about every little thing, not exactly.\n\nIt’s more like there’s this one sore place in me, and it gets touched when I’m dealing with my aunt. If I feel tired, or I feel annoyed, even quietly, even if I don’t say anything mean… I start thinking there must be something wrong with me. Like a good woman wouldn’t feel that way. A better person would just be grateful and patient and loving all the time.\n\nAnd then my mind goes further than it should. It isn’t just, “I’m tired today.” It becomes, “I’m a bad woman,” or, “I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t be more grateful.” So outside of that, not as much. But when it comes to her, yes, I can be very hard on myself, too hard, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself, even when it's not related to your aunt or any specific situation?\n\nPatient: Not really… not if it’s completely separate from my aunt or some situation with her. I know I’ve probably said that a few times, but that really is where it centers for me.\n\nIt’s not like I wake up and criticize myself about everything, or sit there thinking I’m terrible for no reason. It’s more that when I feel tired of taking care of her, or I get annoyed even quietly, that’s when it starts. Then I tell myself, “What kind of woman gets annoyed with her aunt?” or “You should be more grateful.” And it turns into this feeling that I’m a bad woman, like I’m bringing shame on my family just because I’m not feeling patient and thankful enough.\n\nSo if there’s no connection to her and no particular situation like that, I don’t think I’m overly critical in the same way. It’s really those moments with her that make me judge myself so harshly.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally criticize yourself a lot, even when there's nothing in particular bothering you?\n\nPatient: Not usually when there’s nothing bothering me, no. I don’t think I’m just sitting around all day criticizing myself out of nowhere.\n\nIt’s more… it has to get started by that feeling with my aunt. Like if I’m tired and she needs something, or I feel even a little annoyed, then I start turning on myself. I’ll think, “Why am I like this? She’s family. I should be more grateful.” And then it doesn’t stay small. It becomes, “I’m a bad woman,” like there’s something wrong with my character.\n\nAnd I know maybe other people get tired too, but in my mind it feels shameful. Like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I think I’m supposed to be. So… no, not for no reason exactly. But when that comes up, I can be very hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, with these same kinds of thoughts and feelings, at any other time in your life?\n\nPatient: I think… not really in this same way, no. I mean, I’ve felt bad about myself before like anybody does, I suppose, but these particular thoughts — feeling like I’m a bad woman because I’m tired or annoyed with my aunt, and like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t be more grateful — that feels tied to this situation with her.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because it’s not just ordinary guilt, like, “Oh, I should have been nicer.” It has this heavier feeling to it, like it says something about who I am as a person. When I get worn down or impatient, even if I don’t do anything, my mind goes straight to, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then I feel ashamed, like I’ve failed somehow.\n\nSo if you mean have I had this exact kind of thinking at other times in my life, I don’t think so. Not like this. It really seems to come up around taking care of my aunt and not feeling grateful or patient enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I don’t think I remember it being quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had sad times in my life, of course, but this… this feels different in the mornings especially.

Most mornings now I wake up and there’s this heavy sadness sitting in my chest before I’ve even really done anything. It’s like the day has already gone wrong, like I’ve already failed somehow before my feet even touch the floor. I’ll just lie there for a bit with that feeling, and it’s hard to shake.

So if you mean have I had a long stretch before where I woke up feeling that kind of heaviness and sadness over and over… no, not that I can really point to. Not like this current time.

I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll go in knowing I need something simple, like milk or bread, and then I just stand there in front of the shelf too long. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. More than I think I should, really. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. I know I should be grateful to have family, and I know she’s older and needs patience, and I tell myself that. But then there are times when I’m so tired, or she says something and I get annoyed inside, and right away I start thinking, “What kind of woman feels that way?” I don’t even have to say anything out loud. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. I start thinking I’m a bad woman, like I’m not grateful enough, not kind enough, not doing what I should for family. Once I’ve had that thought, it’s hard not to see myself that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself for things you've done in the past, outside of your current situation with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Not so much things from way back, no… at least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think. I see them, and I think, “I should do that,” but I can’t make myself care enough to start.\n\nAnd I used to go to dance class, and I’d enjoy being around people and moving a little, even if I wasn’t the best at it. Same with walking with the ladies. We’d talk and laugh, and I used to like that routine. Lately, though, I just don’t have the interest. I leave my things sitting there, I don’t get ready, I don’t feel any pull toward it. I haven’t been keeping track of it closely. My clothes… I don’t think they fit dramatically different or anything like that. Maybe some days I feel a little heavier or lighter, but I couldn’t tell you if that’s real weight changing or just how I feel in my body that day.\n\nI haven’t stepped on a scale much. I suppose if there has been a change, it hasn’t been something big enough that I noticed and thought, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’ve gained weight.” So… no, I don’t think there’s been any significant change that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling restless and fidgety over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think more slowly, definitely. I didn’t notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to notice it, but my husband has said it a few times now. He’ll say, not in a mean way exactly, but he’ll say, “Everything looks like it takes you so much effort.” Like getting up from the chair, going to get something from the other room, even just starting a simple chore… it’s like I have to talk myself into each little step.\n\nAnd at the market, that’s where I really notice it. I’ll be looking right at what I came for, but I don’t reach for it right away. It’s embarrassing because people go around me, and I feel like I’m in the way, but my body just doesn’t seem to move as quickly as it used to. It’s not exactly being tired in the normal sense… it’s more like everything takes extra pushing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s not always about some big thing I did wrong, it’s more the way I think about myself when I’m not being… I don’t know, kind enough or patient enough.\n\nLike with my aunt. Just having the feeling makes me feel bad.\n\nThen it turns into this whole thing in my head, like I’m a bad woman, like I’m not the kind of person I was raised to be. I start thinking I’m bringing shame on my family because I’m not more grateful, or because I’m not handling things with a better heart. And maybe other people would say, “Everyone gets tired,” but I don’t feel like I can let myself off that easy. It sits on me. I blame myself for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or like you're not a good person?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that way. I don’t know if “worthless” is the exact word all the time, but not good… yes. Like I’m not a good woman, not the kind of person I ought to be.\n\nIt happens especially with my aunt. If I’m tired and she needs something, or if she says something and I feel annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself right away. I think, “What is wrong with me? She’s family. I should be grateful. I should be kinder.” And then it doesn’t just stay as one small feeling. It turns into me thinking I’m bad inside, like I’m bringing shame on my family because I can’t just be patient and thankful the way I should be.\n\nI know maybe other people get irritated too, but when it’s me, it feels like proof that I’m not a good person. It sits with me, and I keep going over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for things beyond just your interactions with your aunt?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes back to my aunt, if I’m being honest. That’s the thing that seems to set it off the strongest. I’ll feel tired, or I’ll get annoyed when she needs something or says something a certain way, and then I start in on myself right away.\n\nIt isn’t just, “Oh, I was impatient.” It becomes bigger than that in my head. It’s mostly those moments with her that make me start criticizing myself like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly critical of yourself in general, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: In general… I don’t know if I’d say it happens all the time, even when things are going well. It’s more like there’s this one place my mind keeps returning to, and that’s with my aunt.\n\nIf I’m having a decent day and then I get tired, or she needs something, or I catch myself feeling annoyed even for a second, I start judging myself very hard. I don’t just think, “I’m tired.” I think, “What kind of woman feels that way about family?” And then it turns into, “I’m a bad woman. I should be more grateful. I’m bringing shame on my family by not having a better heart.”\n\nSo even if things around me are technically fine, that feeling can still come up if it involves her. It makes me feel like there’s something wrong with me as a person, not just that I had a bad moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're overly critical of yourself most of the time, even when it's not about your aunt?\n\nPatient: Most of the time? No… I don’t think it’s most of the time when it has nothing to do with her. If I feel tired, or I feel annoyed, even quietly, even if I don’t say anything mean… I start thinking there must be something wrong with me. Like a good woman wouldn’t feel that way. No, I don’t think I’ve had an episode like this before. I’ve been sad at different times in my life, sure, but not where it settles in like this for a long time.

This time, most mornings I wake up and it’s already there — this heavy sadness in my chest. Before I even get out of bed, it feels like I’ve already failed the day somehow, like the whole day is weighed down before it starts. It’s not just a bad mood that comes and goes quickly. It feels like I’m carrying it into the morning with me, and then I have to try to move through the day with that sitting there.

So… no, not like this. Not that I can remember.
